From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.228, F42.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um... I guess I'm having some trouble at work. I'm a barber, and... well, I've gotten to the point where I'll do almost anything to avoid using a straight razor. \n\nI'll, uh, switch clients with someone else, or... just tell them the blade's missing. I even hide it in my drawer sometimes. And if I really can't get out of it... I just end up shaking through the whole shave. So... yeah. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Is it just the straight razor that you're trying to avoid, or are there other tasks or tools at work that you've been avoiding as well?\n\nPatient: No, just... just the straight razor. I can use the clippers and the shears all day without a problem. It's just... I know it's a tool I trained with, but... when it's sitting there open at my station, I just... I get this feeling like I'm going to hurt somebody. Or... or cut myself really badly. So, yeah. Just that.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear you experience related to the actual act of cutting someone with the straight razor, or is it more about having the razor in your hand or seeing it?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's just seeing it. Or even... just hearing them ask for it. \n\nLike, the second a client asks for a razor shave, or if I just see the blade flipped open... my chest gets tight. My hands get sweaty. It's just... my body wants to back away before I even really think about anything else. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience a similar feeling of fear or anxiety when you're around other sharp objects, like knives or scissors, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just the razor. Knives and stuff are... they're fine. I cook at home a lot, and it doesn't bother me. It's just... just the straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this fear or anxiety when you see a straight razor outside of a barber setting, like at home or in a store?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I don't really see them anywhere else. I mean, they're not exactly sitting around at the grocery store. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious or fearful if you were to hold or see a straight razor in a non-barber setting, such as at home, for a purpose unrelated to shaving someone?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I wouldn't... I don't keep them at my house. There's no reason for me to have one there. So... I couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, just for a moment, with no intention of shaving anyone?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never tried. I just... I avoid them completely if I don't have to use one for work. So, I really couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, with no intention of shaving anyone, just to clean or store it?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I said, I don't have them at home. I just... I don't put myself in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home for a purpose completely unrelated to shaving, such as using it as a prop for a hobby?\n\nPatient: I... I don't have any hobbies like that. Look, I just... I don't bring them into my house for any reason. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by someone else in a non-barber setting, such as in a movie or a play?\n\nPatient: I don't really watch things like that. If something like that comes on the screen, I just... I look away. So I couldn't really tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor to shave another person?\n\nPatient: I... I don't watch other people do that. If one of the other guys at the shop is doing a shave, I just... find a reason to go to the back room. I don't stick around to see how I'd feel.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving, such as cutting a rope?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I just... I try to avoid them altogether, so I haven't been in a situation like that. I just stay away from them.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor in a situation where they were not shaving anyone?\n\nPatient: I... I think that's kind of what you just asked. I don't know. Like I said, I wouldn't be around to see it. If one is out, I just... I walk away.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor in a medical setting, such as a doctor using it for a surgical procedure?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know why a doctor would be using one of those. I've never seen that. But... I mean, I wouldn't be looking. Like I keep saying, I just... Just that.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear you experience related to the actual act of cutting someone with the straight razor, or is it more about having the razor in your hand or seeing it?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's just seeing it. Or even... just hearing them ask for it. \n\nLike, the second a client asks for a razor shave, or if I just see the blade flipped open... my chest gets tight. My hands get sweaty. It's just... my body wants to back away before I even really think about anything else. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience a similar feeling of fear or anxiety when you're around other sharp objects, like knives or scissors, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just the razor. Knives and stuff are... they're fine. I cook at home a lot, and it doesn't bother me. It's just... just the straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this fear or anxiety when you see a straight razor outside of a barber setting, like at home or in a store?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I don't really see them anywhere else. I mean, they're not exactly sitting around at the grocery store. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious or fearful if you were to hold or see a straight razor in a non-barber setting, such as at home, for a purpose unrelated to shaving someone?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I wouldn't... I don't keep them at my house. There's no reason for me to have one there. So... I couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, just for a moment, with no intention of shaving anyone?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never tried. I just... I avoid them completely if I don't have to use one for work. So, I really couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, with no intention of shaving anyone, just to clean or store it?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I said, I don't have them at home. I just... I don't put myself in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home for a purpose completely unrelated to shaving, such as using it as a prop for a hobby?\n\nPatient: I... I don't have any hobbies like that. Look, I just... I don't bring them into my house for any reason. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by someone else in a non-barber setting, such as in a movie or a play?\n\nPatient: I don't really watch things like that. If something like that comes on the screen, I just... I look away. So I couldn't really tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor to shave another person?\n\nPatient: I... I don't watch other people do that. If one of the other guys at the shop is doing a shave, I just... find a reason to go to the back room. I don't stick around to see how I'd feel.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving, such as cutting a rope?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I just... I try to avoid them altogether, so I haven't been in a situation like that. I just stay away from them.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor in a situation where they were not shaving anyone?\n\nPatient: I... I think that's kind of what you just asked. I don't know. Like I said, I wouldn't be around to see it. If one is out, I just... I walk away.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor in a medical setting, such as a doctor using it for a surgical procedure?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know why a doctor would be using one of those. I've never seen that. But... I mean, I wouldn't be looking. Like I keep saying, I just... I don't want to be anywhere near them, no matter who is holding it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by a surgeon in an operating room?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you keep asking me the same thing. I really don't know. \n\nI wouldn't be awake in an operating room anyway. But... if I was, like I just said, I wouldn't look at it. I don't have a different answer for you. I just want to stay away from them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I'm a barber, and... well, I've gotten to the point where I'll do almost anything to avoid using a straight razor. \n\nI'll, uh, switch clients with someone else, or... just tell them the blade's missing. I even hide it in my drawer sometimes. And if I really can't get out of it... I just end up shaking through the whole shave. So... yeah. I can use the clippers and the shears all day without a problem. It's just... I know it's a tool I trained with, but... when it's sitting there open at my station, I just... I get this feeling like I'm going to hurt somebody. Or... or cut myself really badly. So, yeah. Just that.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear you experience related to the actual act of cutting someone with the straight razor, or is it more about having the razor in your hand or seeing it?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's just seeing it. Or even... just hearing them ask for it. \n\nLike, the second a client asks for a razor shave, or if I just see the blade flipped open... my chest gets tight. My hands get sweaty. It's just... my body wants to back away before I even really think about anything else. Just the razor. Knives and stuff are... they're fine. I cook at home a lot, and it doesn't bother me. It's just... just the straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this fear or anxiety when you see a straight razor outside of a barber setting, like at home or in a store?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I don't really see them anywhere else. I mean, they're not exactly sitting around at the grocery store. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious or fearful if you were to hold or see a straight razor in a non-barber setting, such as at home, for a purpose unrelated to shaving someone?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I wouldn't... I don't keep them at my house. There's no reason for me to have one there. So... I couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, just for a moment, with no intention of shaving anyone?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never tried. I just... I avoid them completely if I don't have to use one for work. So, I really couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, with no intention of shaving anyone, just to clean or store it?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I said, I don't have them at home. I just... I don't put myself in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home for a purpose completely unrelated to shaving, such as using it as a prop for a hobby?\n\nPatient: I... I don't have any hobbies like that. Look, I just... I don't bring them into my house for any reason. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by someone else in a non-barber setting, such as in a movie or a play?\n\nPatient: I don't really watch things like that. If something like that comes on the screen, I just... I look away. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um... I guess I'm having some trouble at work. I'm a barber, and... well, I've gotten to the point where I'll do almost anything to avoid using a straight razor. \n\nI'll, uh, switch clients with someone else, or... just tell them the blade's missing. I even hide it in my drawer sometimes. And if I really can't get out of it... I just end up shaking through the whole shave. So... yeah. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Is it just the straight razor that you're trying to avoid, or are there other tasks or tools at work that you've been avoiding as well?\n\nPatient: No, just... just the straight razor. I can use the clippers and the shears all day without a problem. It's just... I know it's a tool I trained with, but... when it's sitting there open at my station, I just... I get this feeling like I'm going to hurt somebody. Or... or cut myself really badly. So, yeah. I guess it's just seeing it. Or even... just hearing them ask for it. \n\nLike, the second a client asks for a razor shave, or if I just see the blade flipped open... my chest gets tight. My hands get sweaty. It's just... my body wants to back away before I even really think about anything else. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience a similar feeling of fear or anxiety when you're around other sharp objects, like knives or scissors, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just the razor. Knives and stuff are... they're fine. I cook at home a lot, and it doesn't bother me. It's just... just the straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this fear or anxiety when you see a straight razor outside of a barber setting, like at home or in a store?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I don't really see them anywhere else. I mean, they're not exactly sitting around at the grocery store. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious or fearful if you were to hold or see a straight razor in a non-barber setting, such as at home, for a purpose unrelated to shaving someone?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I wouldn't... I don't keep them at my house. There's no reason for me to have one there. So... I couldn't tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, just for a moment, with no intention of shaving anyone?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I've never tried. I just... I avoid them completely if I don't have to use one for work. So, I really couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home, with no intention of shaving anyone, just to clean or store it?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I said, I don't have them at home. I just... I don't put myself in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor at home for a purpose completely unrelated to shaving, such as using it as a prop for a hobby?\n\nPatient: I... I don't have any hobbies like that. Look, I just... I don't bring them into my house for any reason. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by someone else in a non-barber setting, such as in a movie or a play?\n\nPatient: I don't really watch things like that. If something like that comes on the screen, I just... I look away. So I couldn't really tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor to shave another person?\n\nPatient: I... I don't watch other people do that. If one of the other guys at the shop is doing a shave, I just... find a reason to go to the back room. I don't stick around to see how I'd feel.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving, such as cutting a rope?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I just... I try to avoid them altogether, so I haven't been in a situation like that. I think that's kind of what you just asked. I don't know. Like I said, I wouldn't be around to see it. If one is out, I just... I walk away.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see someone else using a straight razor in a medical setting, such as a doctor using it for a surgical procedure?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know why a doctor would be using one of those. I've never seen that. But... I mean, I wouldn't be looking. Like I keep saying, I just... I don't want to be anywhere near them, no matter who is holding it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by a surgeon in an operating room?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you keep asking me the same thing. I really don't know. \n\nI wouldn't be awake in an operating room anyway. But... if I was, like I just said, I wouldn't look at it. I don't have a different answer for you. I just want to stay away from them.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used in a completely safe and controlled environment, like a museum exhibit?\n\nPatient: I... look, a museum? I don't... I don't know. I wouldn't go to an exhibit like that in the first place. \n\nIt doesn't really matter if it's behind glass or... or safe, or whatever. If it's there, I'm not. That's... that's all I can tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor that was rusty, old, and clearly unusable?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Rusty or not, it's still... what it is. \n\nIt doesn't make a difference to me. I just wouldn't be around it. I don't really get why we keep going over this.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to see a straight razor being used by someone else for a purpose that has nothing to do with shaving?\n\nPatient: I... I don't think it matters what they're using it for. \n\nIf someone has one, I'm... I'm just going to leave. I don't want to see it. I don't want to think about it. I just... I really don't want to talk about these things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to hold a straight razor with the intention of giving yourself a shave?\n\nPatient: I... I don't shave with one. I just... I wouldn't do that. I can't even think about holding one right now. Can we please just move on?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
